People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t want to make too much of it, doctor, but I’ve been having some thoughts that worry my family. I keep thinking my wife and the kids might be better off if I didn’t wake up. And I’ve said a few times lately that I don’t really need to see another winter. I’m sorry, that sounds awful… I just didn’t know where else to go.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would harm yourself if you were to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I… I haven’t really wanted to spell it out, if that makes sense. Mostly it’s been more like thinking my wife and the kids might be better off if I just didn’t wake up. I’ve said I don’t need to see another winter… but I’m sorry, I don’t want to make this sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: How is your mood been lately?\n\nPatient: It’s been… not great, I suppose. I don’t want to overstate it. I have my okay moments, but mostly I’ve felt pretty low. Heavy, maybe. I try not to complain too much, though. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low mood?\n\nPatient: I guess… if I’m being honest, it’s been since my bowling partner died. That was about a year and a month ago now. I kept thinking it would lift after a while, you know, but it’s sort of stayed with me. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying activities or hobbies that you typically find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I used to look forward to things… bridge, working in the garden, helping with the cooking at church, even going to Cubs games. Now I mostly just sit in my chair, and I can’t seem to make myself care about any of it. I don’t mean to sound ungrateful… but that’s how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in pleasure from activities you used to enjoy over the past year?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I used to look forward to bridge, tending the garden, helping with the church cooking, even going to Cubs games. Lately I mostly just sit in my chair, and I can’t seem to make myself care about much of it. Is that the sort of decrease you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor. I haven’t been keeping close track of my weight. My clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I couldn’t tell you if it’s a significant change. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: A little slower, I suppose… though I don’t want to overstate it. My wife says I move like I’m walking through deep snow. Even getting dressed or just crossing the kitchen seems to take me such a long time, and sometimes I give up before I’ve done much of anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I have. I don’t like saying it, it sounds self-pitying, but I keep going back over things I did wrong—with him, and with my family. Little things, old things… they just keep coming up.\n\nAnd I get to feeling like I’m not much good to anybody anymore. Like I deserve whatever punishment is coming, if that makes sense. Is that… the sort of guilt you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low and having thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been quite a while. Since my bowling partner died, really. That was about a year and a month ago now.\n\nI don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s when I remember it starting, the feeling low and… those thoughts about not waking up. Is that what you mean by how long?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean… I used to look forward to things—bridge, working in the garden, helping with the cooking at church, even going to Cubs games.\n\nNow I mostly just sit in my chair. People suggest those things, and I know I ought to care, but I just… can’t seem to make myself feel interested. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that you still enjoy, like a favorite food or activity?\n\nPatient: Not much, if I’m honest. I don’t want to sound ungrateful—food is fine, and people try to bring me things—but I don’t really look forward to it.\n\nI used to enjoy bridge, my garden, cooking at church, going to Cubs games… little things like that. Now I mostly just end up sitting in my chair, and I can’t seem to make myself care about any of it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days, most of the day, I just don’t care the way I used to.\n\nI used to look forward to bridge, working in the garden, helping with the church cooking, even watching or going to Cubs games. Now I mostly just sit in my chair, and I can’t seem to make myself feel interested in any of it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Has this low mood you're describing been continuous since your bowling partner died, or have you had periods where you felt better in between?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty much since he died, yes. I don’t want to overstate it, but I can’t really point to a time where I felt back to myself in between.\n\nThat was… about a year and a month ago now, I think. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Is your low mood something that you experience for most of the day, or is it more intermittent?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more… most of the day, if I’m being honest. I may have little moments where I’m distracted, or I answer someone and seem okay, but it doesn’t really lift for long.\n\nI don’t want to make too much of it, but it’s sort of there in the background nearly all the time. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this low feeling you're describing been going on for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: No… not more than two years. It’s been since my bowling partner died, so about a year and a month now, give or take. I hope I’m remembering that clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Is there any part of the day where you consistently feel better, or is the low mood pretty consistent throughout?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a reliable time when it’s much better. Maybe if something distracts me for a bit it feels a little lighter, but I wouldn’t say there’s a part of the day I can count on feeling okay.\n\nI hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Is that what you mean by how long?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean… I used to look forward to things—bridge, working in the garden, helping with the cooking at church, even going to Cubs games.\n\nNow I mostly just sit in my chair. People suggest those things, and I know I ought to care, but I just… can’t seem to make myself feel interested. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that you still enjoy, like a favorite food or activity?\n\nPatient: Not much, if I’m honest. I don’t want to sound ungrateful—food is fine, and people try to bring me things—but I don’t really look forward to it.\n\nI used to enjoy bridge, my garden, cooking at church, going to Cubs games… little things like that. Now I mostly just end up sitting in my chair, and I can’t seem to make myself care about any of it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days, most of the day, I just don’t care the way I used to.\n\nI used to look forward to bridge, working in the garden, helping with the church cooking, even watching or going to Cubs games. Now I mostly just sit in my chair, and I can’t seem to make myself feel interested in any of it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Has this low mood you're describing been continuous since your bowling partner died, or have you had periods where you felt better in between?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty much since he died, yes. I don’t want to overstate it, but I can’t really point to a time where I felt back to myself in between.\n\nThat was… about a year and a month ago now, I think. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Is your low mood something that you experience for most of the day, or is it more intermittent?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more… most of the day, if I’m being honest. I may have little moments where I’m distracted, or I answer someone and seem okay, but it doesn’t really lift for long.\n\nI don’t want to make too much of it, but it’s sort of there in the background nearly all the time. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this low feeling you're describing been going on for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: No… not more than two years. It’s been since my bowling partner died, so about a year and a month now, give or take. I hope I’m remembering that clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Is there any part of the day where you consistently feel better, or is the low mood pretty consistent throughout?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a reliable time when it’s much better. Maybe if something distracts me for a bit it feels a little lighter, but I wouldn’t say there’s a part of the day I can count on feeling okay.\n\nI hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Has your low mood lasted for two years or more?\n\nPatient: No, not two years or more. It’s been… since my bowling partner died, so about a year and a month. I may be off a little, but that’s about right, I think.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
